   121	
   122	def find_lle_files(trigger):
   123	    """Return (lle_event_file, pointing_file, rsp_file) or (None, None, None).
   124	    from_lat_lle REQUIRES the LLE POINTING file gll_pt_*.fit; the LAT FT2
   125	    (gll_ft2_*) makes it FitFail (LATBright E2E audit C1-C3), so gll_pt is used
   126	    STRICTLY and gll_ft2 is only a last resort with a warning. A common file
   127	    version is preferred across the triplet to avoid mixed-version DRMs."""
   128	    import re
   129	    base = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, trigger)
   130	    def _v(p):
   131	        m = re.search(r'_v(\d+)\.', os.path.basename(p)); return m.group(1) if m else None
   132	    lle = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(base, 'gll_lle_*.fit*')))
   133	    pt = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(base, 'gll_pt_*.fit*')))
   134	    ft2 = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(base, 'gll_ft2_*.fit*')))
   135	    rsp = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(base, 'gll_lle_*.rsp*'))
   136	               + glob.glob(os.path.join(base, 'gll_cspec_*.rsp*')))
   137	    if not (lle and (pt or ft2) and rsp):
   138	        return None, None, None
   139	    L = lle[-1]; v = _v(L)
   140	    if pt:
   141	        P = next((p for p in pt if _v(p) == v), pt[-1])
   142	    else:
   143	        P = ft2[-1]
   144	        print(f'    {trigger}: WARNING no gll_pt POINTING file — falling back to LAT '
   145	              f'FT2 {os.path.basename(P)} (from_lat_lle may FitFail)')
   146	    R = next((r for r in rsp if _v(r) == v), rsp[-1])
   147	    return L, P, R

   150	def load_brightest_bins(trigger, single_path):
   151	    sp = Table.read(single_path, format='ascii.ecsv')
   152	    m = sp['TRIGGER_NAME'] == trigger
   153	    if not m.any():
   154	        return None
   155	    return str(sp[m][0]['DETECTOR']).strip()
   156	
   157	
   158	def get_canonical_bins(trigger, bb_spectral_path, single_path, approved_dets):
   159	    bb = Table.read(bb_spectral_path, format='ascii.ecsv')
   160	    bb = bb[bb['TRIGGER_NAME'] == trigger]
   161	    if len(bb) == 0:
   162	        raise RuntimeError(f'No BB blocks in {bb_spectral_path} for {trigger}')
   163	
   164	    dets_in_bb = sorted(set(str(r['DETECTOR']).strip() for r in bb)
   165	                        & set(approved_dets))
   166	    if not dets_in_bb:
   167	        raise RuntimeError('No overlap between BB ECSV dets and approved bkg dets')
   168	
   169	    brightest = load_brightest_bins(trigger, single_path)
   170	    if brightest in dets_in_bb:
   171	        canonical_det = brightest
   172	    else:
   173	        det_scores = {d: float(bb[bb['DETECTOR'] == d]['SIGNIFICANCE'].mean())
   174	                      for d in dets_in_bb}
   175	        canonical_det = max(det_scores, key=det_scores.get)
   176	
   177	    canonical = bb[bb['DETECTOR'] == canonical_det]
   178	    canonical.sort('T_START')
   179	    return canonical_det, list(canonical['T_START']), list(canonical['T_STOP'])

   187	# SOURCE interval natively; if that genuinely cannot cover the source bins, the

   191	def build_spectrumlike_per_block(trigger, det, pre, post, bin_starts, bin_stops):
   192	    from threeML import TimeSeriesBuilder
   193	    if det == 'lle':
   194	        # LAT LLE — 30-100 MeV native; uses separate event/FT2/RSP triplet
   195	        lle_file, ft2_file, rsp_file = find_lle_files(trigger)
   196	        if lle_file is None:
   197	            return None
   198	        try:
   199	            tsb = TimeSeriesBuilder.from_lat_lle(
   200	                'lle', lle_file=lle_file, ft2_file=ft2_file,
   201	                rsp_file=rsp_file, verbose=False)
   202	        except Exception as exc:
   203	            print(f'    lle: from_lat_lle failed — {exc}')
   204	            return None
   205	        sel_args, sel_kwargs = LLE_RANGES, {}
   206	    else:
   207	        tte = find_tte(trigger, det)
   208	        rsp = find_rsp(trigger, det)
   209	        if tte is None or rsp is None:
   210	            return None
   211	        tsb = TimeSeriesBuilder.from_gbm_tte(det, tte, rsp_file=rsp,
   212	                                              verbose=False)
   213	        if det.startswith('n'):
   214	            sel_args, sel_kwargs = NAI_RANGES, {'exclude': list(NAI_EXCLUDE)}
   215	        else:
   216	            sel_args, sel_kwargs = BGO_RANGES, {}
   217	
   218	    # The polynomial background fit can raise FitFailed for one detector
   219	    # (degenerate bkg window / sparse channel). Skip that detector rather than
   220	    # aborting the whole burst — the joint fit proceeds on the others.
   221	    try:
   222	        # Background-fit mode PINNED via native kwargs so a user-level threeML
   223	        # config cannot silently alter/remove the polynomial background
   224	        # (Codex 3ML audit 2026-07-24; auto order selection = poly_order -1).
   225	        tsb.set_background_interval(f'{pre[0]:.3f}-{pre[1]:.3f}',

    55	**Evidence.** Every model is fit once (`scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:601-612`); restart logic exists only for the two +BB models (`scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:614-662`). Current outputs contain 132/992 finite DSBPL-vs-SBPL likelihood differences below -1, including 67 below -9.2 and a minimum of -395, which cannot represent the best optimum of the more flexible model.
    56	
    57	**Impact.** The reported 14/108 = 13% decisive two-break fraction at the locked threshold is a lower limit of unknown size. Failures are concentrated enough to affect which high-S/N bins are testable.
    58	
    59	**Fix.** Add DSBPL multi-starts seeded from the converged SBPL and physically ordered break grids, require the child optimum not to be worse than the parent beyond tolerance, and rerun the complete sample.
    60	
    61	### H7. The nu_m-nu_c headline uses double-break parameters even when a second break is not required
    62	
    63	**Evidence.** Any physically valid DSBPL contributes `xb` and `xp`, regardless of its improvement over SBPL (`scripts/31_draft_numbers.py:116-120`), and the population fit uses all finite pairs (`scripts/31_draft_numbers.py:238-256`). The D'Agostini likelihood also omits the covariance between the two break estimates even though they come from the same fit (`scripts/31_draft_numbers.py:222-236`).
    64	
    65	**Impact.** The paper quotes rho=0.57, N=349 and slope 0.46+/-0.05 (`paper/two_break.tex:462-466`). Requiring the stated decisive second break leaves **26 pairs**, not 349, with rho=0.69; requiring both decisiveness and <100% marginal errors leaves 23 pairs with rho=0.75. The correlation survives, but its denominator and physical interpretation change substantially, and the slope/scatter uncertainty is understated without the within-fit covariance.
    66	
    67	**Fix.** Label the 349-point result as an exploratory all-fit relation, make the decisive/constrained subset primary for physical claims, and store/use the joint covariance of `(xb,xp)` in the regression.
    68	
    69	### H8. rsp2 fallback replaces time-dependent responses with one matrix for entire bursts
    70	
    71	**Evidence.** On any response-coverage failure, the engine selects the single matrix with largest overlap with the full source span (`scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:135-164`) and then uses that static response for every time bin (`scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:204-228`). Current logs show this fallback for every detector of bn090829672, bn100130729, and bn150721242.
    72	
    73	**Impact.** 32/1,057 bins across three bursts were fit with responses that need not correspond to each bin's spacecraft orientation. The impact on their parameters and model choices is unquantified.
    74	
    75	**Fix.** Build a response per bin from the overlapping rsp2 matrices, or restrict each SpectrumLike to a matrix that covers that bin; record the selected matrix interval in metadata.
    76	
    77	### H9. The locked Delta-AIC=10 framework is not the headline result
    78	
    79	**Evidence.** The README locks decisiveness at Delta-AIC >=10 (`README.md:129-140`) but leads with the Delta-AIC>6 split (`README.md:17-21`). The population script computes both (`scripts/31_draft_numbers.py:176-186`), and the paper likewise headlines the >6 result (`paper/two_break.tex:435-445`).
    80	
    81	**Impact.** The headline changes from **151/166 = 91% thermal-or-degenerate and 9% two-break** to the locked result **94/108 = 87% and 14/108 = 13%**: 58 fewer qualifying bins and a 4 percentage-point increase in the two-break fraction.
    82	
    83	**Fix.** Use the >=10 split in the abstract, README, summary, and conclusions; retain >6 only as a labeled sensitivity analysis.
    84	
    85	### H10. Fine-grid variability p0 is not calibrated for the data representation used
    86	
    87	**Evidence.** Script 35 passes one row per 1-2 ms grid cell plus integer counts to Astropy's `fitness='events'` (`scripts/35_variability_bb.py:41-57`). In this API, the change-point prior is calibrated using the number of supplied time cells, not the number of photon events. Changing the arbitrary grid therefore changes both the candidate cells and the nominal p0 penalty; the method is not the grid-independent event-arrival analysis claimed in the docstring (`scripts/35_variability_bb.py:3-11`).
    88	
    89	**Impact.** The paper's **11/106 bursts below 128 ms and 2 ms minimum** (`paper/two_break.tex:208-212`) is not a statistically calibrated false-alarm result and should not be used to validate 128 ms production binning.
    90

135:def _collapse_rsp2_to_single(rsp2_path, src_lo, src_hi, out_dir):
136:    """Some GBM .rsp2 files have time-dependent matrices that only span the
137-    trigger window; when the analysis background interval falls outside that
138-    span, 3ML's count-weighted response build raises IntervalOfInterestNotCovered.
139:    Extract the single SPECRESP MATRIX with the largest overlap with the SOURCE
140-    window [src_lo, src_hi] and write it as a standalone .rsp (PRIMARY + EBOUNDS
141-    + that matrix). Returns the temp .rsp path, or None on failure.
142-    The static single matrix is the standard treatment for short/time-integrated
143-    GBM spectra and avoids the time-weighting that needs full coverage."""
144-    try:
145:        with fits.open(rsp2_path) as h:
146-            trigt = next((e.header['TRIGTIME'] for e in h
147-                          if 'TRIGTIME' in e.header), None)
148-            mats = [i for i, e in enumerate(h) if e.name == 'SPECRESP MATRIX']
149-            if len(mats) <= 1:
150-                return None       # genuinely single matrix; nothing to collapse
151-            best, best_ov = mats[0], -1.0
152-            for i in mats:
153-                t0 = h[i].header.get('TSTART'); t1 = h[i].header.get('TSTOP')
154-                if t0 is None or t1 is None or trigt is None:
155-                    continue
156-                ov = max(0.0, min(t1 - trigt, src_hi) - max(t0 - trigt, src_lo))
157-                if ov > best_ov:
158-                    best_ov, best = ov, i
159-            sel = fits.HDUList([h[0], h['EBOUNDS'], h[best]])
160-            sel[2].header['EXTVER'] = 1
161:            base = os.path.basename(rsp2_path).replace('.rsp2', '')
162-            out = os.path.join(out_dir, f'_single_{base}.rsp')
163-            sel.writeto(out, overwrite=True)
164-        return out
165-    except Exception:
166-        return None

    20	class Interval:
    21	    def __init__(self, start: float, stop: float, swap_if_inverted: bool = False):
    22	        self._start: float = float(start)
    23	        self._stop: float = float(stop)
    24	
    25	        # Note that this allows to have intervals of zero duration
    26	
    27	        if self._stop < self._start:
    28	            if swap_if_inverted:
    29	                self._start: float = stop
    30	                self._stop: float = start
    31	
    32	            else:
    33	                log.exception(
    34	                    "Invalid time interval! TSTART must be before TSTOP and "
    35	                    "TSTOP-TSTART >0. Got tstart = %s and tstop = %s" % (start, stop)
    36	                )
    37	
    38	                raise RuntimeError()
    39	
    40	    @property
    41	    def start(self) -> float:
    42	        return self._start
    43	
    44	    @property
    45	    def stop(self) -> float:
    46	        return self._stop
    47	
    48	    @classmethod
    49	    def new(cls, *args, **kwargs):
    50	        return cls(*args, **kwargs)
    51	
    52	    def _get_width(self) -> float:
    53	        return self._stop - self._start
    54	
    55	    @property
    56	    def mid_point(self) -> float:
    57	        return (self._start + self._stop) / 2.0
    58	
    59	    def __repr__(self):
    60	        return " interval %s - %s (width: %s)" % (
    61	            self.start,
    62	            self.stop,
    63	            self._get_width(),
    64	        )
    65	
    66	    def intersect(self, interval):
    67	        # type: (Interval) -> Interval
    68	        """Returns a new time interval corresponding to the intersection
    69	        between this interval and the provided one.
    70	
    71	        :param interval: a TimeInterval instance
    72	        :type interval: Interval
    73	        :return: new interval covering the intersection
    74	        :raise IntervalsDoNotOverlap : if the intervals do not overlap
    75	        """
    76	
    77	        if not self.overlaps_with(interval):
    78	            log.exception("Current interval does not overlap with provided interval")
    79	            raise IntervalsDoNotOverlap()
    80	
    81	        new_start = max(self._start, interval.start)
    82	        new_stop = min(self._stop, interval.stop)
    83	
    84	        return self.new(new_start, new_stop)
    85	
    86	    def merge(self, interval):
    87	        # type: (Interval) -> Interval
    88	        """Returns a new interval corresponding to the merge of the current and
    89	        the provided time interval. The intervals must overlap.
    90	
    91	        :param interval: a TimeInterval instance
    92	        :type interval : Interval
    93	        :return: a new TimeInterval instance
    94	        """
    95	
    96	        if self.overlaps_with(interval):
    97	            new_start = min(self._start, interval.start)
    98	            new_stop = max(self._stop, interval.stop)
    99	
   100	            return self.new(new_start, new_stop)
   101	
   102	        else:
   103	            raise IntervalsDoNotOverlap("Could not merge non-overlapping intervals!")
   104	
   105	    def overlaps_with(self, interval):
   106	        # type: (Interval) -> bool
   107	        """Returns whether the current time interval and the provided one
   108	        overlap or not.
   109	
   110	        :param interval: a TimeInterval instance
   111	        :type interval: Interval
   112	        :return: True or False
   113	        """
   114	
   115	        if interval.start == self._start or interval.stop == self._stop:
   116	            return True
   117	
   118	        elif interval.start > self._start and interval.start < self._stop:
   119	            return True
   120	
   121	        elif interval.stop > self._start and interval.stop < self._stop:
   122	            return True
   123	
   124	        elif interval.start < self._start and interval.stop > self._stop:
   125	            return True
   126	
   127	        else:
   128	            return False

   934	
   935	    def _get_weighted_matrix(self, switch: str, *intervals) -> InstrumentResponse:
   936	        if not len(intervals) > 0:
   937	            log.error("You have to provide at least one interval")
   938	
   939	            raise RuntimeError()
   940	
   941	        intervals_set = TimeIntervalSet.from_strings(*intervals)
   942	
   943	        # Compute a set of weights for each interval
   944	        weights = np.zeros(len(self._matrix_list))
   945	
   946	        for interval in intervals_set:
   947	            weights += self._weight_response(interval, switch)
   948	
   949	        # Normalize to 1
   950	        weights /= np.sum(weights)
   951	
   952	        # Weight matrices
   953	        matrix = np.dot(
   954	            np.array(list(map(attrgetter("matrix"), self._matrix_list))).T,
   955	            weights.T,

   957

   964	        mc_channels = self._matrix_list[0].monte_carlo_energies
   965	
   966	        matrix_instance = InstrumentResponse(matrix, ebounds, mc_channels)
   967

   969	
   970	    def _weight_response(
   971	        self, interval_of_interest: TimeInterval, switch: str
   972	    ) -> np.ndarray:
   973	        """
   974	
   975	        :param interval_start : start time of the interval
   976	        :param interval_stop : stop time of the interval
   977	        :param switch: either 'counts' or 'exposure'
   978	
   979	        """
   980	
   981	        #######################
   982	        # NOTE: the weights computed here are *not* normalized to one so that they can
   983	        # be combined if there is more than one interval
   984	        #######################
   985	
   986	        # Now mark all responses which overlap with the interval of interest
   987	        # NOTE: this is a mask of the same length as _matrix_list and
   988	        # _coverage_intervals
   989	
   990	        matrices_mask = [
   991	            c_i.overlaps_with(interval_of_interest) for c_i in self._coverage_intervals
   992	        ]
   993

  1037	                        this_effective_interval.stop_time,

   797	def model_columns(spec, result, n_data):
   798	    """Flatten a fit result into ECSV columns prefixed with spec['prefix']."""
   799	    p = spec['prefix']
   800	    out = {f'{p}_STATUS': result.get('status', 'NA'),
   801	           f'{p}_N2LL':   result.get('neg2logL', float('nan')),
   802	           f'{p}_MINOS_OK': bool(result.get('minos_ok', False)),
   803	           # honest labels (Codex 3ML audit): MINOS_OK only means get_errors
   804	           # returned a table (per-side validity flags are not propagated by
   805	           # threeML); the symmetric 'error' column is covariance-MC equal-tail,
   806	           # NOT a direct Hessian sigma. AIC here is RAW AIC (-2logL + 2k),
   807	           # not threeML's AICc.
   808	           f'{p}_ERROR_METHOD': ('MINOS' if result.get('minos_ok', False)
   809	                                 else 'COVARIANCE_MC'),
   810	           f'{p}_VALID': bool(result.get('physical', False))}
   811	    if result.get('status') == 'OK':
   812	        n2ll = result['neg2logL']
   813	        # k = true free-parameter count incl. EAC/LAT nuisances when available
   814	        nk = result.get('n_params_total') or result['n_params']
   815	        out[f'{p}_AIC'] = n2ll + 2 * nk
   816	        out[f'{p}_BIC'] = n2ll + nk * np.log(max(n_data, 1))
   817	    else:
   818	        out[f'{p}_AIC'] = float('nan')
   819	        out[f'{p}_BIC'] = float('nan')
   820	    # peak-shape statistics from the fitted curve (NaN when peak not interior)
   821	    out[f'{p}_EPK_CURVE'] = result.get('epk_curve', float('nan'))
   822	    out[f'{p}_WIDTH_HM'] = result.get('width_hm', float('nan'))
   823	    for col_suffix, short in spec['pmap'].items():
   824	        d = result.get('params', {}).get(short)
   825	        if d is None:
   826	            out[f'{p}_{col_suffix}']         = float('nan')
   827	            out[f'{p}_{col_suffix}_ERR']     = float('nan')
   828	            out[f'{p}_{col_suffix}_NEG_ERR'] = float('nan')
   829	            out[f'{p}_{col_suffix}_POS_ERR'] = float('nan')
   830	        else:
   831	            out[f'{p}_{col_suffix}']         = d['val']
   832	            out[f'{p}_{col_suffix}_ERR']     = d['err']
   833	            out[f'{p}_{col_suffix}_NEG_ERR'] = d['neg']
   834	            out[f'{p}_{col_suffix}_POS_ERR'] = d['pos']
   835	    return out

  1024	    """Apply selection rules across all OK fits.
  1025	
  1026	    - BEST_AIC: lowest AIC (Burnham-Anderson; for non-nested empirical models)
  1027	    - BEST_BIC: lowest BIC (cross-check)
  1028	    - LRT_BANDBB_BAND, LRT_CPLBB_CPL: nested-pair LRTs (Wilks)
  1029	    """
  1030	    # OK requires a FINITE likelihood — a fit whose objective read back NaN
  1031	    # must not be selectable (Codex ultra audit HIGH #16).
  1032	    ok = {sp['name']: (sp, r) for sp, r in per_spec_results
  1033	          if r.get('status') == 'OK' and np.isfinite(r.get('neg2logL', np.nan))}
  1034	    aic = {n: r['neg2logL'] + 2 * r['n_params'] for n, (_, r) in ok.items()}
  1035	    bic = {n: r['neg2logL'] + r['n_params'] * np.log(max(n_data, 1))
  1036	           for n, (_, r) in ok.items()}
  1037	    # Physical-validity gate: the winner must be a non-railed, physically
  1038	    # ordered fit (DSBPL low break xb < peak xp). Railed/inverted fits stay
  1039	    # in the ECSV but cannot WIN selection — and when NO fit passes the gate,
  1040	    # the block is INCONCLUSIVE. The old fallback silently crowned the raw
  1041	    # invalid minimum (Codex ultra audit HIGH #16: bn110721200 block 0 had
  1042	    # every candidate invalid yet BEST_AIC_MODEL=SBPL).
  1043	    phys = {n: (sp, r) for n, (sp, r) in ok.items() if _fit_is_physical(sp, r)}
  1044	    aic_p = {n: aic[n] for n in phys}
  1045	    bic_p = {n: bic[n] for n in phys}
  1046	    best_aic = min(aic_p, key=aic_p.get) if aic_p else 'INCONCLUSIVE'
  1047	    best_bic = min(bic_p, key=bic_p.get) if bic_p else 'INCONCLUSIVE'

  1049	    def _lrt(parent, child):
  1050	        if parent in ok and child in ok:
  1051	            return ok[parent][1]['neg2logL'] - ok[child][1]['neg2logL']
  1052	        return float('nan')

  1061	    # to zero); LRT < -0.5 means the child fit failed to converge and MUST NOT

   924	# L28 (2026-08-11; Tierney+2013 2013A&A...550A.102T, Ravasio+2019
   925	# 2019A&A...625A..60R App. B): a component/break is constrained by its in-band
   926	# nuFnu turnover — 3.92*kT for a blackbody, xb for a low break — not by data at
   927	# its nominal energy. Ravasio's App. B shows GBM fits with the feature below
   928	# ~20 keV produce railed, unphysically hard alpha1 (their Fig. B.1 bimodality)
   929	# and they quarantine those from population statistics. We stamp instead of
   930	# drop: EDGE_CONSTRAINED (<20 keV) / EDGE_MARGINAL (20-30 keV, up to the
   931	# Tierney LET / K-edge region) / IN_BAND (>=30 keV). Stamped values require
   932	# the L28 checks (LET-extrapolation, per-NaI coherence, L25 identity pair)
   933	# before entering any record.
   934	EDGE_TRUST_KEV = 20.0      # Ravasio+2019 App. B empirical boundary
   935	EDGE_CLEAR_KEV = 30.0      # above this the turnover is comfortably in-band
   936	BB_PEAK_FACTOR = 3.92      # nuFnu peak of a Planck spectrum = 3.92 kT
   937	
   938	
   939	def edge_feature_class(kt=None, xb=None):
   940	    """Classify an edge-proximal feature by its nuFnu turnover energy (keV).
   941	
   942	    Pass EITHER kt (blackbody temperature) OR xb (low-break energy); per L25
   943	    they are one feature in two costumes and must classify identically.
   944	    Returns (class_str, feature_kev).
   945	    """
   946	    if kt is not None and np.isfinite(kt):
   947	        feat = BB_PEAK_FACTOR * float(kt)
   948	    elif xb is not None and np.isfinite(xb):
   949	        feat = float(xb)
   950	    else:
   951	        return 'NO_FEATURE', float('nan')
   952	    if feat < EDGE_TRUST_KEV:
   953	        return 'EDGE_CONSTRAINED', feat
   954	    if feat < EDGE_CLEAR_KEV:
   955	        return 'EDGE_MARGINAL', feat
   956	    return 'IN_BAND', feat
   957	
   958	
   959	def _fit_is_physical(spec, result, frac=0.001):
   960	    """A fit may WIN model selection only if it is OK, has no key shape
   961	    parameter railed within `frac` of a bound, and (for DSBPL) the low break
   962	    xb < the peak xp. Railed / inverted fits stay in the ECSV (with VALID=False)
   963	    but are excluded from BEST_AIC / BEST_BIC so the declared winner is always
   964	    a physical solution. Burgess+ 2019 pre-publication railing filter + Ravasio
   965	    physical-ordering requirement for the 2SBPL break."""
   966	    if result.get('status') != 'OK':
   967	        return False
   968	    prefix = spec['prefix']
   969	    pmap = spec['pmap']                     # col_suffix -> short param name
   970	    params = result.get('params', {})
   971	    for col, (lo, hi) in PARAM_BOUNDS.get(prefix, {}).items():
   972	        short = pmap.get(col)
   973	        if short is None:
   974	            continue
   975	        d = params.get(short)
   976	        if d is None or not np.isfinite(d['val']):
   977	            return False
   978	        v = d['val']
   979	        # L27 (bn090530760, 2026-08-10): the rail test must respect the
   980	        # parameter's GEOMETRY. A linear margin frac*(hi-lo) on a log-spanning
   981	        # bound like (30, 5e4) is ~50 keV of dead zone above the LOW bound —
   982	        # every Ep below ~80 keV was auto-flagged railed, disqualifying all
   983	        # simple models on soft bursts and biasing selection toward
   984	        # extra-component winners. For bounds spanning >2 decades with lo>0,
   985	        # test in LOG space (margin = 1% of the log-span, e.g. 30 -> 32.3 keV);
   986	        # keep the linear rule for narrow/linear bounds (indices, kT).
   987	        if lo > 0 and hi / lo > 100.0:
   988	            lg_lo, lg_hi, lg_v = np.log10(lo), np.log10(hi), np.log10(v)
   989	            lg_m = 0.01 * (lg_hi - lg_lo)
   990	            if (lg_v - lg_lo) < lg_m or (lg_hi - lg_v) < lg_m:
   991	                return False
   992	        else:
   993	            span = hi - lo
   994	            if (v - lo) < frac * span or (hi - v) < frac * span:
   995	                return False
   996	    if prefix in ('DSBPL', 'DSBPLF'):
   997	        xb = params.get('xb'); xp = params.get('xp')
   998	        if (xb and xp and np.isfinite(xb['val']) and np.isfinite(xp['val'])
   999	                and xb['val'] >= xp['val']):
  1000	            return False
  1001	    return True
  1002	
  1003

   680	census would systematically prefer extra components in soft bursts — a selection bias
   681	masquerading as physics. No previous burst felt it (all had Ep ≳ 250 keV): **the defect was
   682	invisible until serial ordering delivered a soft burst.**
   683	**RULE:** bounds spanning > 2 decades (lo > 0) are tested in **log space** — margin = 1% of the
   684	log-span (30 → 32.3 keV; 5×10⁴ → 4.6×10⁴). Narrow/linear bounds (indices, kT) keep the linear
   685	rule. More generally: any threshold applied to a parameter must live in the parameter's natural
   686	geometry.
   687	**TEST:** `test_L27_rail_margin_respects_log_geometry` (unit-level, imports the gate directly).
   688	
   689	### L28 — a feature below ~20 keV is EDGE-CONSTRAINED: stamp it and earn it, never quote it bare  *(the low-kT night class, 2026-08-11; anchors: Tierney+2013 `2013A&A...550A.102T`, Ravasio+2019 `2019A&A...625A..60R` App. B — both version-of-record on disk)*
   690	A component is constrained by its **in-band νFν turnover** (3.92·kT for a blackbody, xb for a
   691	low break), not by data at its nominal energy — so kT ≈ 4–6 keV from NaI data starting at 8 keV
   692	is legitimate *in principle* (Tierney's Table 4 publishes Band+BB with kT = 4.99 ± 0.5 keV for
   693	GRB 090323). But the literature brackets the edge from both sides:
   694	- **Ravasio+2019 App. B (the trust boundary):** GBM fits with Ebreak < 20 keV produce railed,
   695	  unphysically hard α₁ (their 171010: Ebreak = 12.4 keV, α₁ = **+1.16**), splitting the
   696	  Ebreak–α₁ plane into two non-overlapping populations at ~20 keV — "strongly suggests an
   697	  instrumental effect"; they exclude everything below 20 keV from population statistics.
   698	- **Tierney+2013 (how to earn an edge feature):** full-band C-stat *smears* deviations through
   699	  the model; their extrapolated-fit method (fit above a Low-Energy Threshold, extrapolate to
   700	  8 keV, test the coherent residual run below the LET against ~1000 response-aware simulations)
   701	  is the defensible localization test. Both excesses AND deficits occur (a component between
   702	  LET and Ep flattens α → deficit).
   703	- **The unresolved two-costume tension:** Ravasio's "instrumental" α₁ ≈ +1.16 at a ~12 keV break
   704	  is numerically the Rayleigh–Jeans photon index (+1) — what one lineage quarantines as artifact
   705	  is what the other would call a photosphere. Tierney's 090424 is fit both as Band+BB (kT ~ 9)
   706	  and as a double-broken PL (Ebreak1 = 32.7 keV). The identity question is OPEN in the
   707	  literature; we must not close it by labeling.
   708	- **Artifacts come in two classes** (Vikas, 2026-08-11): *detector-level* (blockage, bad
   709	  response — lives in ONE NaI; killed by per-NaI coherence, D5 pointed in reverse) and
   710	  *shared-primitive* (common DRM generator, background family, fit statistic — coherent across
   711	  ALL NaIs, like the Ravasio pathology; killed only by simulations or a different instrument).
   712	  Per-NaI coherence ACQUITS the first class only.
   713	
   714	**RULE — any significant component/break with turnover (3.92·kT or xb) below 30 keV:**
   715	1. stamp via `edge_feature_class`: **EDGE_CONSTRAINED** (< 20 keV) / **EDGE_MARGINAL**
   716	   (20–30 keV); stamped values never enter population statistics unstamped — we *report*
   717	   (data-dropping hierarchy), Ravasio-style quarantine is for the stats, not the record;
   718	2. rail check on the paired low index (α₁ > −0.2 = the Fig. B.1 signature);
   719	3. **LET-extrapolation localization** (Tierney): continuum fit above ~30 keV only, extrapolate
   720	   down, coherent residual run 8→LET; simulation-calibrated before any promoted claim;
   721	4. **per-NaI coherence, amplitude-weighted**: the excess in EVERY NaI at the amplitude its own
   722	   DRM predicts; per-detector kT consistency where counts allow; a low-S/N detector's silence
   723	   is NOT incoherence;
   724	5. background-fit sanity specifically in 8–30 keV over the source window;
   725	6. **L25 identity pair**: report BB margin AND DSBPL-low-break margin side by side; if both
   726	   adequate the record says "a low-energy spectral feature at ~E keV" — never "a blackbody";
   727	7. cross-instrument (BAT/XRT overlap) where it exists — the only true test of the
   728	   shared-response class.
   729	Cuts BOTH ways: a census DSBPL with **xb < 20 keV** carries the same stamp — this lesson
   730	protects the two-break count itself, not just the BB class. Mirrored HET version for the
   731	above-peak project lives in the #34 registry entry.
   732	**ENGINE:** `edge_feature_class(kt=, xb=)` in scripts/10 (EDGE_TRUST_KEV = 20, EDGE_CLEAR_KEV
   733	= 30, BB_PEAK_FACTOR = 3.92) — pure classifier, no fit behavior change.
   734	**TEST:** `test_L28_edge_feature_class` (unit-level; includes the L25 consistency property —
   735	kt and xb = 3.92·kt must classify identically).
   320	            f'Ep={ep} keV should be flagged as railed')

   323	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   324	# L28 (2026-08-11; Tierney+2013 2013A&A...550A.102T, Ravasio+2019
   325	# 2019A&A...625A..60R App. B): a feature is constrained by its in-band nuFnu
   326	# turnover (3.92*kT or xb). Ravasio's App. B shows GBM fits with the feature
   327	# below ~20 keV are artifact-prone (railed hard alpha1); such values are
   328	# stamped EDGE_CONSTRAINED and must pass the L28 checks before entering any
   329	# record or population statistic. Unit-test the classifier directly.
   330	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   331	def test_L28_edge_feature_class():
   332	    import importlib.util
   333	    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
   334	        's10', os.path.join(BASE, 'scripts', '10_spectral_fit_burst.py'))
   335	    s10 = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
   336	    spec.loader.exec_module(s10)
   337	    fc = s10.edge_feature_class
   338	    # the night-shift low-kT class: turnover deep below the trust boundary
   339	    assert fc(kt=3.0)[0] == 'EDGE_CONSTRAINED'      # 11.8 keV (b16 tail, b17)
   340	    assert fc(kt=4.5)[0] == 'EDGE_CONSTRAINED'      # 17.6 keV (b11, b15)
   341	    # kT 5.6-6.1 -> 22-24 keV: above Ravasio's boundary, below the clear zone
   342	    assert fc(kt=6.1)[0] == 'EDGE_MARGINAL'
   343	    # comfortably in-band temperatures stay unstamped
   344	    assert fc(kt=10.0)[0] == 'IN_BAND'              # 39.2 keV
   345	    assert fc(kt=41.0)[0] == 'IN_BAND'              # the high-kT class (b18)
   346	    # published anchor cases, break costume
   347	    assert fc(xb=12.4)[0] == 'EDGE_CONSTRAINED'     # Ravasio 171010 (quarantined)
   348	    assert fc(xb=93.6)[0] == 'IN_BAND'              # Ravasio 180720 Fig. 1
   349	    # L25 one-feature consistency: kt and xb = 3.92*kt classify identically,
   350	    # and the reported feature energy is the same number
   351	    for kt in (2.0, 4.4, 5.1, 6.1, 9.0, 25.0):
   352	        c_kt, e_kt = fc(kt=kt)
   353	        c_xb, e_xb = fc(xb=3.92 * kt)
   354	        assert c_kt == c_xb and abs(e_kt - e_xb) < 1e-9, (
   355	            f'kT={kt}: BB and break costumes classify differently (L25 breach)')
   356	    # degenerate inputs fail closed
   357	    assert fc()[0] == 'NO_FEATURE'
   358	    assert fc(kt=float('nan'))[0] == 'NO_FEATURE'

   472	            mc_channels = self._read_mc_channels(data)
  1054	            log.error(
  1055	                "All weights are zero. There must be a bug in the exposure or counts "
  1056	                "computation"
  1057	            )
  1058	
  1059	            raise RuntimeError()
  1060	
  1061	        # Check that the first matrix with weight > 0 has an effective interval starting
  1062	        # at the beginning of the interval of interest (otherwise it means that part of
  1063	        # the interval of interest is not covered!)
  1064	
  1065	        if effective_intervals[0].start_time != interval_of_interest.start_time:
  1066	            log.error(
  1067	                "The interval of interest (%s) start is not covered by %s"
  1068	                % (interval_of_interest, effective_intervals[0])
  1069	            )
  1070	
  1071	            raise IntervalOfInterestNotCovered()
  1072	
  1073	        # Check that the last matrix with weight > 0 has an effective interval starting
  1074	        # at the beginning of the interval of interest (otherwise it means that part of
  1075	        # the interval of interest is not covered!)
    14

 succeeded in 0ms:
    64	
    65	**Impact.** The paper quotes rho=0.57, N=349 and slope 0.46+/-0.05 (`paper/two_break.tex:462-466`). Requiring the stated decisive second break leaves **26 pairs**, not 349, with rho=0.69; requiring both decisiveness and <100% marginal errors leaves 23 pairs with rho=0.75. The correlation survives, but its denominator and physical interpretation change substantially, and the slope/scatter uncertainty is understated without the within-fit covariance.
    66	
    67	**Fix.** Label the 349-point result as an exploratory all-fit relation, make the decisive/constrained subset primary for physical claims, and store/use the joint covariance of `(xb,xp)` in the regression.
    68	
    69	### H8. rsp2 fallback replaces time-dependent responses with one matrix for entire bursts
    70	
    71	**Evidence.** On any response-coverage failure, the engine selects the single matrix with largest overlap with the full source span (`scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:135-164`) and then uses that static response for every time bin (`scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:204-228`). Current logs show this fallback for every detector of bn090829672, bn100130729, and bn150721242.
    72	
    73	**Impact.** 32/1,057 bins across three bursts were fit with responses that need not correspond to each bin's spacecraft orientation. The impact on their parameters and model choices is unquantified.
    74	
    75	**Fix.** Build a response per bin from the overlapping rsp2 matrices, or restrict each SpectrumLike to a matrix that covers that bin; record the selected matrix interval in metadata.
    76	
    77	### H9. The locked Delta-AIC=10 framework is not the headline result
    78

The population script computes both (`scripts/31_draft_numbers.py:176-186`), and the paper likewise headlines the >6 result (`paper/two_break.tex:435-445`).

## MUST-FIX

1. **The night blackbody scorecard omits the CPL+BB nested test.** The engine emits both `LRT_BANDBB_BAND` and `LRT_CPLBB_CPL` ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1054](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1054)), but every night track is Band+BB-only ([walkthrough_night_summary.json:7](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/results/walkthrough_night_summary.json:7)). Result: **31 reported versus 55 qualifying burst-blocks**, with 24 CPL+BB-only blocks omitted.  
   **Fix:** Form the union of qualifying Band+BB and CPL+BB nested pairs and retain the model label with each kT.

2. **L28 is documented as mandatory but is not connected to fit records or population products.** The classifier exists at [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:939](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:939), while the guide itself calls it a “pure classifier, no fit behavior change” ([SpectralFitting.md:732](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:732)); no production call or serialized edge-class column exists.  
   **Fix:** Serialize feature energy/class for valid significant BB and low-break fits, then enforce the stamped quarantine in population scripts.

3. **b12 loses every valid block because one uncovered bin invalidates the detector’s entire batch.** One `to_spectrumlike` call covers all bins and returns `[None] * n_bins` after any exception ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:231](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:231)); the fit loop consequently reports no plugins ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1684](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1684)).  
   **Fix:** Detect response coverage per bin and retry/build only covered bins; preserve blocks 1–7 and explicitly mark block 0/T_INT unavailable.

4. **L28 materially misstates both papers’ methods.** Ravasio is described as finding “railed” and “non-overlapping” populations ([SpectralFitting.md:694](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:694)); the paper instead says hard, ill-constrained indices and *almost* no overlap. Tierney is reduced to fitting “above ~30 keV” and testing a “coherent residual run” ([SpectralFitting.md:698](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:698)); the actual protocol scans LETs `{15,20,25,30,50,100}` keV and uses a summed-residual statistic averaged across detectors.  
   **Fix:** Replace those passages with the papers’ exact terminology and LET/statistic procedure; label coherent-run logic as a project extension.

5. **The documented kT rail geometry contradicts executable behavior.** The guide and code comment say kT remains linear ([SpectralFitting.md:683](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:683), [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:984](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:984)), but `(1,200)` satisfies `hi/lo > 100`, so the code applies the logarithmic cutoff, kT ≥ **1.0544119 keV** ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:987](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:987)).  
   **Fix:** Define rail geometry explicitly per parameter and make documentation and boundary tests match it.

## SHOULD-CONSIDER

1. **The 30 keV “comfortably in-band” boundary is a project heuristic, not a result of either paper.** It is especially awkward because the analysis excludes NaI 33–40 keV ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:67](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:67)). Transferring Ravasio’s 2SBPL 20 keV boundary to `3.92·kT` is likewise an inference.  
   **Fix:** Label both the BB transfer and 30 keV boundary as project policy and calibrate them with model-specific simulations.

2. **P0 mtimes establish “before summary,” not a strong blind-first chain.** Every fit table already existed before its P0 file; all nine P0 files are untracked, and `frozen_utc` is free text rather than a precise timestamp ([bn180728728 P0:4](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/notes/reconciliation/bn180728728_P0_frozen.json:4)).  
   **Fix:** Create, hash, and commit an append-only P0 manifest before fit execution, with exact UTC and source hashes.

3. **Four archival denominators call T_INT a block.** The P0 priors `6/22`, `3/19`, `5/18`, and `3/18` include `BLOCK=-1`, which the engine defines as time-integrated ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1672](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1672)); resolved denominators are 21, 18, 17, and 17.  
   **Fix:** Label these as “rows including T_INT” or recompute resolved-block fractions.

4. **The BB significance rule does not state whether the child must be physically valid.** Under the literal requested LRT+kT rule, three blocks qualify solely through an invalid child: b11/20, b16/11, b20/2. The engine otherwise treats `*_VALID` as the physical-selection gate ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:959](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:959)).  
   **Fix:** State whether `child_VALID=True` is required; if so, the audit union becomes 52 rather than 55 blocks.

5. **The summary schema changes halfway through the nine bursts.** Entries b11–b16 use `sig_kT_blocks`/`ep_trend`, while b17–b20 use `sig_kT`/`ep` ([walkthrough_night_summary.json:247](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/results/walkthrough_night_summary.json:247)).  
   **Fix:** Normalize the JSON keys and add a schema/version field.

6. **L28’s record policy is internally contradictory.** The guide says retain and report stamped values ([SpectralFitting.md:714](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:714)); code/test comments say checks are required before entering *any* record ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:931](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:931), [test_lessons.py:324](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/tests/test_lessons.py:324)).  
   **Fix:** Standardize on “retain stamped in records; exclude from population/promotion until checks pass.”

7. **L28 tests omit exact boundaries and conflicting inputs.** Current tests cover representative values but not exactly 20/30 keV, and `edge_feature_class(kt=..., xb=...)` silently prioritizes kT despite documenting “EITHER” ([test_lessons.py:331](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/tests/test_lessons.py:331)).  
   **Fix:** Test exact equalities and reject simultaneous finite `kt` and `xb`.

8. **The stated response preflight was not applied to b12.** The inventory guide already requires testing each response against the stamped source window ([DataInventory.md:23](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/DataInventory.md:23)).  
   **Fix:** Run that preflight before bin fitting and emit coverage bounds in the run manifest.

## NOTE

1. **The enumerated numerical paper anchors are accurate.** Tierney Table 4 gives 090323 kT `4.99 +0.53/-0.52` keV and 090424 kT `9.20 +0.55/-0.44` keV; Fig. 8 gives 090424 `Ebreak1=32.74 +1.56/-1.55` keV ([Tierney PDF](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/Skills_training/Tierney_2013_2013AA550A102T.pdf)). Ravasio gives 171010 `Ebreak=12.39±0.13` keV and `α1=+1.16±0.13` ([Ravasio PDF](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/Skills_training/Ravasio_2019_2019AA625A60R.pdf)).  
   **Fix:** None for the numbers.

2. **The 3.92 factor is correct but is not stated in either cited paper.** The local blackbody definition is \(N(E)\propto E^2/(\exp(E/kT)-1)\) ([blackbody.py:22](/Users/salim/anaconda3/envs/threeML/lib/python3.9/site-packages/astromodels/functions/functions_1D/blackbody.py:22)); maximizing \(E^2N(E)\) gives \(x=E/kT=3.9206904\).  
   **Fix:** Cite it as an analytic derivation, not a Tierney/Ravasio quotation.

3. **The local Tierney PDF is an arXiv v1/preprint artifact, not the claimed version-of-record** ([SpectralFitting.md:689](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:689)).  
   **Fix:** Correct the provenance label or replace it with the publisher PDF.

4. **The b12 `KeyError: ("MATRIX",N)` is a benign fallback warning.** threeML first tries `MATRIX` and then intentionally reads `SPECRESP MATRIX` ([response.py:438](/Users/salim/anaconda3/envs/threeML/lib/python3.9/site-packages/threeML/utils/OGIP/response.py:438)).  
   **Fix:** Do not rename HDUs or restore the historical collapse workaround.

## Task 1 — blind re-derivation and full diff

### Frozen engine interpretation

The highe tables expose 24 model prefixes:

`BAND, CPL, SBPL, DSBPL, BANDBB, CPLBB, SBPLF, DSBPLF, BANDPL, BANDCPL, CPLPL, CPLCPL, BANDRCPL, BANDCUT, SBPLCUT, SBPLPL, SBPLCPL, BANDBBPL, BANDBBCPL, CPLBBPL, CPLBBCPL, SBPLBB, SBPLBBPL, SBPLBBCPL`.

The base/shape definitions start at [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:437](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:437) and [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:484](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:484).

I classified the engine’s explicitly named simple-restart family as the simple/one-break set:

`{BAND, CPL, SBPL, SBPLF}`

This is directly supported by `SIMPLE_RESTART_SEEDS` ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1161](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1161)). The other 20 prefixes are the extra-structure set, including DSBPL/DSBPLF, additive components, and cutoff variants.

`*_VALID` is the physical gate and `*_AIC = N2LL + 2k` ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:797](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:797)). I used the minimum finite AIC among valid models in each partition and computed:

\[
\Delta AIC=AIC_{\rm best\ simple}-AIC_{\rm best\ extra}.
\]

`BLOCK=-1` is T_INT and was excluded. No other negative block indices occur.

The BB-bearing `*_KT` prefixes are `BANDBB, CPLBB, BANDBBPL, BANDBBCPL, CPLBBPL, CPLBBCPL, SBPLBB, SBPLBBPL, SBPLBBCPL`, but the engine exposes nested BB LRTs only for `BANDBB:BAND` and `CPLBB:CPL`. The requested literal rule therefore uses those two pairs.

|---|---|

| Approximately 1000 simulations | **Verified:** approximately 1000 per parameter set; response folding is part of the simulation. |
| Full-band fits smear/balance deviations; excesses and deficits occur | **Supported.** |
| Ravasio 20 keV boundary | **Verified as an empirical 2SBPL boundary.** Its application to BB peaks is a project inference. |

## Task 4 — b12 root cause and minimal fix

All five v00 RSP2 files are structurally valid: 17 HDUs, `DRM_NUM=15`, one `EBOUNDS`, and 15 `SPECRESP MATRIX` extensions with `EXTVER=1…15`. Direct loading succeeds.

The actual failure chain is:

1. Approved source begins at `−1.2 s` ([background_intervals.ecsv:189](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/results/background_intervals.ecsv:189)); block 0 begins at `−0.803679943 s` ([b12 blocks:15](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/results/walkthrough_b12/blocks/bb_blocks_spectral_bn150721242.ecsv:15)).

2. The first response begins at trigger-relative `+0.063966 s`, so block 0 and T_INT are not fully covered.

3. threeML therefore raises `IntervalOfInterestNotCovered` at its explicit coverage check ([response.py:1061](/Users/salim/anaconda3/envs/threeML/lib/python3.9/site-packages/threeML/utils/OGIP/response.py:1061)).

4. Because all eight bins are converted together, that one exception discards plugins for blocks 1–7 even though they are response-covered. Converting blocks 1–7 alone succeeds for all seven.

The quoted `KeyError: ("MATRIX",N)` is only the logged first lookup; threeML then reads `SPECRESP MATRIX`. There is no persisted b12 fit log under `results/walkthrough_b12/`, only the block catalog.

The historical single-matrix collapse is not a safe fix. It selected one DRM for the whole source and silently extrapolated it across uncovered time; that bias is already documented at [CODEX_AUDIT_REPORT_PIPELINE.md:69](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/CODEX_AUDIT_REPORT_PIPELINE.md:69) and its removal at [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:182](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:182).

Minimal safe remedy: retain native RSP2 handling, preflight coverage, construct spectrum-like objects only for covered bins, and fit blocks 1–7. For an authoritative full-burst result, regenerate/acquire a response covering the approved negative-time source edge or revise/reapprove and reblock that edge; do not fabricate coverage.

## Five-line executive summary

1. DECISIVE/STRONG scorecard counts are fully reproduced: all 18 burst-level fields agree.
2. The BB census is incomplete: Band-only bookkeeping reports 31 blocks, while the two engine-defined nested pairs yield 55.
3. L28’s core numerical anchors are sound, but parts of its source attribution are overstated and the classifier is not wired into outputs.
4. All nine P0 files predate downstream summaries and show no current-fit leakage, but their untracked post-fit creation weakens blind-first provenance.
5. b12 is a response-coverage batching bug—not a malformed RSP2—and blocks 1–7 are recoverable without restoring the biased collapse fallback.

Read-only audit complete; no files were changed. Task 1’s rules, counts, and tracks were frozen before any scorecard, night summary, or reconciliation file was opened.

## MUST-FIX

1. **The night blackbody scorecard omits the CPL+BB nested test.** The engine emits both `LRT_BANDBB_BAND` and `LRT_CPLBB_CPL` ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1054](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1054)), but every night track is Band+BB-only ([walkthrough_night_summary.json:7](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/results/walkthrough_night_summary.json:7)). Result: **31 reported versus 55 qualifying burst-blocks**, with 24 CPL+BB-only blocks omitted.  
   **Fix:** Form the union of qualifying Band+BB and CPL+BB nested pairs and retain the model label with each kT.

2. **L28 is documented as mandatory but is not connected to fit records or population products.** The classifier exists at [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:939](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:939), while the guide itself calls it a “pure classifier, no fit behavior change” ([SpectralFitting.md:732](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:732)); no production call or serialized edge-class column exists.  
   **Fix:** Serialize feature energy/class for valid significant BB and low-break fits, then enforce the stamped quarantine in population scripts.

3. **b12 loses every valid block because one uncovered bin invalidates the detector’s entire batch.** One `to_spectrumlike` call covers all bins and returns `[None] * n_bins` after any exception ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:231](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:231)); the fit loop consequently reports no plugins ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1684](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1684)).  
   **Fix:** Detect response coverage per bin and retry/build only covered bins; preserve blocks 1–7 and explicitly mark block 0/T_INT unavailable.

4. **L28 materially misstates both papers’ methods.** Ravasio is described as finding “railed” and “non-overlapping” populations ([SpectralFitting.md:694](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:694)); the paper instead says hard, ill-constrained indices and *almost* no overlap. Tierney is reduced to fitting “above ~30 keV” and testing a “coherent residual run” ([SpectralFitting.md:698](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:698)); the actual protocol scans LETs `{15,20,25,30,50,100}` keV and uses a summed-residual statistic averaged across detectors.  
   **Fix:** Replace those passages with the papers’ exact terminology and LET/statistic procedure; label coherent-run logic as a project extension.

5. **The documented kT rail geometry contradicts executable behavior.** The guide and code comment say kT remains linear ([SpectralFitting.md:683](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:683), [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:984](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:984)), but `(1,200)` satisfies `hi/lo > 100`, so the code applies the logarithmic cutoff, kT ≥ **1.0544119 keV** ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:987](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:987)).  
   **Fix:** Define rail geometry explicitly per parameter and make documentation and boundary tests match it.

## SHOULD-CONSIDER

1. **The 30 keV “comfortably in-band” boundary is a project heuristic, not a result of either paper.** It is especially awkward because the analysis excludes NaI 33–40 keV ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:67](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:67)). Transferring Ravasio’s 2SBPL 20 keV boundary to `3.92·kT` is likewise an inference.  
   **Fix:** Label both the BB transfer and 30 keV boundary as project policy and calibrate them with model-specific simulations.

2. **P0 mtimes establish “before summary,” not a strong blind-first chain.** Every fit table already existed before its P0 file; all nine P0 files are untracked, and `frozen_utc` is free text rather than a precise timestamp ([bn180728728 P0:4](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/notes/reconciliation/bn180728728_P0_frozen.json:4)).  
   **Fix:** Create, hash, and commit an append-only P0 manifest before fit execution, with exact UTC and source hashes.

3. **Four archival denominators call T_INT a block.** The P0 priors `6/22`, `3/19`, `5/18`, and `3/18` include `BLOCK=-1`, which the engine defines as time-integrated ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1672](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1672)); resolved denominators are 21, 18, 17, and 17.  
   **Fix:** Label these as “rows including T_INT” or recompute resolved-block fractions.

4. **The BB significance rule does not state whether the child must be physically valid.** Under the literal requested LRT+kT rule, three blocks qualify solely through an invalid child: b11/20, b16/11, b20/2. The engine otherwise treats `*_VALID` as the physical-selection gate ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:959](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:959)).  
   **Fix:** State whether `child_VALID=True` is required; if so, the audit union becomes 52 rather than 55 blocks.

5. **The summary schema changes halfway through the nine bursts.** Entries b11–b16 use `sig_kT_blocks`/`ep_trend`, while b17–b20 use `sig_kT`/`ep` ([walkthrough_night_summary.json:247](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/results/walkthrough_night_summary.json:247)).  
   **Fix:** Normalize the JSON keys and add a schema/version field.

6. **L28’s record policy is internally contradictory.** The guide says retain and report stamped values ([SpectralFitting.md:714](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:714)); code/test comments say checks are required before entering *any* record ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:931](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:931), [test_lessons.py:324](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/tests/test_lessons.py:324)).  
   **Fix:** Standardize on “retain stamped in records; exclude from population/promotion until checks pass.”

7. **L28 tests omit exact boundaries and conflicting inputs.** Current tests cover representative values but not exactly 20/30 keV, and `edge_feature_class(kt=..., xb=...)` silently prioritizes kT despite documenting “EITHER” ([test_lessons.py:331](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/tests/test_lessons.py:331)).  
   **Fix:** Test exact equalities and reject simultaneous finite `kt` and `xb`.

8. **The stated response preflight was not applied to b12.** The inventory guide already requires testing each response against the stamped source window ([DataInventory.md:23](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/DataInventory.md:23)).  
   **Fix:** Run that preflight before bin fitting and emit coverage bounds in the run manifest.

## NOTE

1. **The enumerated numerical paper anchors are accurate.** Tierney Table 4 gives 090323 kT `4.99 +0.53/-0.52` keV and 090424 kT `9.20 +0.55/-0.44` keV; Fig. 8 gives 090424 `Ebreak1=32.74 +1.56/-1.55` keV ([Tierney PDF](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/Skills_training/Tierney_2013_2013AA550A102T.pdf)). Ravasio gives 171010 `Ebreak=12.39±0.13` keV and `α1=+1.16±0.13` ([Ravasio PDF](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/Skills_training/Ravasio_2019_2019AA625A60R.pdf)).  
   **Fix:** None for the numbers.

2. **The 3.92 factor is correct but is not stated in either cited paper.** The local blackbody definition is \(N(E)\propto E^2/(\exp(E/kT)-1)\) ([blackbody.py:22](/Users/salim/anaconda3/envs/threeML/lib/python3.9/site-packages/astromodels/functions/functions_1D/blackbody.py:22)); maximizing \(E^2N(E)\) gives \(x=E/kT=3.9206904\).  
   **Fix:** Cite it as an analytic derivation, not a Tierney/Ravasio quotation.

3. **The local Tierney PDF is an arXiv v1/preprint artifact, not the claimed version-of-record** ([SpectralFitting.md:689](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/ai_guides/SpectralFitting.md:689)).  
   **Fix:** Correct the provenance label or replace it with the publisher PDF.

4. **The b12 `KeyError: ("MATRIX",N)` is a benign fallback warning.** threeML first tries `MATRIX` and then intentionally reads `SPECRESP MATRIX` ([response.py:438](/Users/salim/anaconda3/envs/threeML/lib/python3.9/site-packages/threeML/utils/OGIP/response.py:438)).  
   **Fix:** Do not rename HDUs or restore the historical collapse workaround.

## Task 1 — blind re-derivation and full diff

### Frozen engine interpretation

The highe tables expose 24 model prefixes:

`BAND, CPL, SBPL, DSBPL, BANDBB, CPLBB, SBPLF, DSBPLF, BANDPL, BANDCPL, CPLPL, CPLCPL, BANDRCPL, BANDCUT, SBPLCUT, SBPLPL, SBPLCPL, BANDBBPL, BANDBBCPL, CPLBBPL, CPLBBCPL, SBPLBB, SBPLBBPL, SBPLBBCPL`.

The base/shape definitions start at [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:437](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:437) and [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:484](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:484).

I classified the engine’s explicitly named simple-restart family as the simple/one-break set:

`{BAND, CPL, SBPL, SBPLF}`

This is directly supported by `SIMPLE_RESTART_SEEDS` ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1161](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:1161)). The other 20 prefixes are the extra-structure set, including DSBPL/DSBPLF, additive components, and cutoff variants.

`*_VALID` is the physical gate and `*_AIC = N2LL + 2k` ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:797](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:797)). I used the minimum finite AIC among valid models in each partition and computed:

\[
\Delta AIC=AIC_{\rm best\ simple}-AIC_{\rm best\ extra}.
\]

`BLOCK=-1` is T_INT and was excluded. No other negative block indices occur.

The BB-bearing `*_KT` prefixes are `BANDBB, CPLBB, BANDBBPL, BANDBBCPL, CPLBBPL, CPLBBCPL, SBPLBB, SBPLBBPL, SBPLBBCPL`, but the engine exposes nested BB LRTs only for `BANDBB:BAND` and `CPLBB:CPL`. The requested literal rule therefore uses those two pairs.

## Task 2 — paper-by-paper verification

| Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|
| 090323 Band+BB kT = 4.99±0.5 keV | **Verified.** Tierney Table 4: `4.99 +0.53/-0.52`. |
| 090424 kT ≈9 keV | **Verified.** Table 4: `9.20 +0.55/-0.44`. |
| 090424 DBPL Ebreak1 =32.7 keV | **Verified.** Fig. 8: `32.74 +1.56/-1.55`. |
| Tierney LET values | **Verified:** 15, 20, 25, 30, 50, 100 keV. The lesson’s single “~30 keV” description is inaccurate. |
| Approximately 1000 simulations | **Verified:** approximately 1000 per parameter set; response folding is part of the simulation. |
| Full-band fits smear/balance deviations; excesses and deficits occur | **Supported.** |
| Ravasio 20 keV boundary | **Verified as an empirical 2SBPL boundary.** Its application to BB peaks is a project inference. |
| 171010 Ebreak and α1 | **Verified:** `12.39±0.13` keV and `+1.16±0.13`. |
| Quarantine below-20 breaks from population statistics | **Verified in substance.** Population distributions/means retain the >20 keV subset. |
| “Railed,” exactly “non-overlapping” | **Not supported.** The paper says hard/ill-constrained and almost no overlap. |
| 3.92·kT νFν peak | **Mathematically verified**, but not supplied by either cited paper. |
| 20/30 classifier boundaries | **Implementation matches lesson exactly:** `<20`, `[20,30)`, and `≥30` ([scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:952](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:952)). |
| Rayleigh–Jeans “two-costume tension” | Numerically plausible project inference; neither paper makes that cross-identification. |
| Per-NaI/shared-primitive artifact taxonomy and “only true test” language | Project protocol, not supported as a claim from these two papers; “only” is overreach. |

## Task 4 — b12 root cause and minimal fix

All five v00 RSP2 files are structurally valid: 17 HDUs, `DRM_NUM=15`, one `EBOUNDS`, and 15 `SPECRESP MATRIX` extensions with `EXTVER=1…15`. Direct loading succeeds.

The actual failure chain is:

1. Approved source begins at `−1.2 s` ([background_intervals.ecsv:189](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/results/background_intervals.ecsv:189)); block 0 begins at `−0.803679943 s` ([b12 blocks:15](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/results/walkthrough_b12/blocks/bb_blocks_spectral_bn150721242.ecsv:15)).

2. The first response begins at trigger-relative `+0.063966 s`, so block 0 and T_INT are not fully covered.

3. threeML therefore raises `IntervalOfInterestNotCovered` at its explicit coverage check ([response.py:1061](/Users/salim/anaconda3/envs/threeML/lib/python3.9/site-packages/threeML/utils/OGIP/response.py:1061)).

4. Because all eight bins are converted together, that one exception discards plugins for blocks 1–7 even though they are response-covered. Converting blocks 1–7 alone succeeds for all seven.

The quoted `KeyError: ("MATRIX",N)` is only the logged first lookup; threeML then reads `SPECRESP MATRIX`. There is no persisted b12 fit log under `results/walkthrough_b12/`, only the block catalog.

The historical single-matrix collapse is not a safe fix. It selected one DRM for the whole source and silently extrapolated it across uncovered time; that bias is already documented at [CODEX_AUDIT_REPORT_PIPELINE.md:69](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/dev/CODEX_AUDIT_REPORT_PIPELINE.md:69) and its removal at [scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:182](/Users/salim/Desktop/Projects/SingleRest/Two_Breaks/scripts/10_spectral_fit_burst.py:182).

Minimal safe remedy: retain native RSP2 handling, preflight coverage, construct spectrum-like objects only for covered bins, and fit blocks 1–7. For an authoritative full-burst result, regenerate/acquire a response covering the approved negative-time source edge or revise/reapprove and reblock that edge; do not fabricate coverage.

## Five-line executive summary

1. DECISIVE/STRONG scorecard counts are fully reproduced: all 18 burst-level fields agree.
2. The BB census is incomplete: Band-only bookkeeping reports 31 blocks, while the two engine-defined nested pairs yield 55.
3. L28’s core numerical anchors are sound, but parts of its source attribution are overstated and the classifier is not wired into outputs.
4. All nine P0 files predate downstream summaries and show no current-fit leakage, but their untracked post-fit creation weakens blind-first provenance.
5. b12 is a response-coverage batching bug—not a malformed RSP2—and blocks 1–7 are recoverable without restoring the biased collapse fallback.
